ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e2449	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e2464	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Among the outrages of the President of the Federal Republic of Germany, the year 1996 has been declared the year of the National Parks. That is how we today, looking back and fortunately, have actually become. The project of the Danube National Park is due to the conclusion of the State Treaty between the countries of Vienna, Lower Austria and the German Confederation, which was represented by me. In October, it was finally realized. This has resulted in a particularly important national park project that has needed more than ten years of preparation. In addition, the Kalkalpen National Park project in Upper Austria has also been implemented so far that I was able to sign the corresponding state agreement with the Upper Austrian head of state Dr. Pühringer in the first few days of 1997. This also makes this national park project well in progress. If I may sum up briefly: Today we have four national parks in Austria: the Hochgebirge National Park Hohe Tauern, the steppe national park Neusiedler See/Seewinkel, the unique Auennational Park Donauen and the Waldnational Park Kalkalpen. I believe, ladies and gentlemen, that we can be proud of this together.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e2506	Federal Minister! Apart from the National Park Projects, the themes of nature conservation and landscape protection at the federal level unfortunately only have little popularity. The state reform is now being discussed again. Would you, as Minister for the Environment, be committed to ensuring that, in the course of these negotiations, there is also a federal competence for nature conservation?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e2524	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e2539	Mrs Langthaler! It is one of the principles of our state that the countries remain certain competences, and this certainly includes nature conservation competence. Only in the field of so-called IUCN criteria 1 and 2, i.e. the international nature conservation criteria for the highest levels – in some cases – in which the national parks also fall, is the Federation able to act and finance nature conservation tasks in half. In addition, in the context of wetlands, we are only able to promote nature conservation projects on a point-by-point basis. In addition, however, I do not think of affecting the proven nature conservation competence of the countries or trying to establish, for example, a federal competence. There is no intention or conversation in this sense.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e2604	Mr Barmüller! Inconceivable is nothing in politics. It is just not realistic at all. I don't see any reason to do that either. National Park Projects will continue to be developed in Austria on the basis of voluntaryness and commonness. ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e4000	Mr President. Deputies! The promotion of talent, the promotion of talent is a major concern. We have already set up the department or department for gift promotion last year. A brochure on gift promotion was prepared by the Ministry. There have been numerous measures in schools for several years, for example in the field of Olympics and competitions. I recall the innovation competition "Youth Innovative", in which especially talented young people can submit projects and projects are awarded the award accordingly. Last year, for example, 260 projects were submitted for this competition alone, "Youth Innovative". The winner project is a device for automatic pollen analysis, which enables allergy sufferers to immediately detect the pollen in the air. The relevant fields of research and science have also been interested in this. There are plus courses, there are offers from the economy. I would like to strengthen the following measures. First of all, we are dealing with the recognition of gifts. It is extremely important to recognize the talent. Secondly, it is important to allow for the overlap of classes, also in folk school, but with all caution. Children must not be overwhelmed either. Thirdly, talented young people in the gymnasium or in secondary schools are to be given the opportunity to go to universities in advance, attend lectures and take exams there. In the last few weeks, in discussions with university professors, I have found that there is great willingness to cooperate and that advanced study parts can be recognized for the study. I am also particularly concerned that individual gifts can be promoted. I would like to look for a way to create special emphasis in the context of a gift fund that is fed out of private resources. For example, a musically particularly talented youth could get an education from a good musician or make another internship in the business sector. The field of gift promotion is an extremely important area. This has been done for years. I have set a special priority, and we will continue to do so.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e8187	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Today's agenda also includes a request on the promotion of talent in the school for discussion. Ladies and gentlemen! Paragraph 2 of the School Organisation Act, also known as the target section of the schools, clearly describes the task of the Austrian school. In it you can read – I quote –: "The Austrian school has the task of participating in the development of youth's facilities according to moral, religious and social values, as well as in the values of the true, good and beautiful, through an education corresponding to its development stage and its educational path. It must equip young people with the knowledge and skills necessary for life and future occupations and train them to acquire self-employed and self-employed education. Young people are to be educated into healthy, labour-intensive, responsible and responsible members of society and citizens of the democratic and federal Republic of Austria. They are intended to lead to independent judgments, to social understanding, to the political and worldly thinking of others, and to be able to take part in the economic and cultural life of Austria, Europe and the world." Thus, it is clear that it is the task of the Austrian schools to give the corresponding education to all pupils, regardless of their origin, origin and gender. So it is also a task to promote the student within the class according to his talent. In recent years, the Federal Minister Gehrer has implemented and implemented many reforms in the school system. It has thus helped to improve the Austrian school system. It is certainly not surprising, for example, that a few months ago an investigation found that Austria's pupils are simply a leader. ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e10546	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! In order to make the related problem of this question understandable, I think it is necessary, first of all, to present a brief description of the sale of shares by the Republic of Austria to Bank Austria AG. ==References== The purchase contract concluded by the Republic with Bank Austria AG in January 1997 is a suspensive condition. This is because transactions of this type must be approved by the antitrust authority before they are carried out. In this case, the competent antitrust authority shall be that of the EU Commission. There is currently a merger control procedure. Until the decision of the EU Commission, the Republic is the owner of the shares. However, the purchase price of 17,2 billion Schilling has already been applied to a blocked account of the Republic. My predecessor has entrusted a trustee with the exercise of the rights arising from the share rights. In the purchase agreement with the Republic, Bank Austria AG has committed itself to the following offer to minority shareholders: "The buyer will submit an exchange offer to all other shareholders of the CA within 3 years from the legal acquisition of the CA shares of the federal government in bank-austria shares, subject to reasonable application of the valuation principles of §§ 220 ff of the Stock Exchange Act. Vote-based small shareholders (physical persons with a stock holding up to 200 000 S with protection rules against abuse) are made an alternative cash collection offer, at least at the rate of 850 minus a maximum of 15 percent." Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I would, however, like to say in general on this problem that there are no legal provisions in Austria which provide for the removal of minority shareholders in the event of takeovers. However, there are no final directives of the European Union in this direction either. For this reason, there has been no resolution of minority shareholders in Austria, such as the Radex/Veischer or the BBAG/Gösser-Reininghaus in recent transactions. It is therefore initially specified that the offer of the Bank Austria was a voluntary offer. However, I would like to return once again to the text of my predecessor, Mr Peter, whom you have quoted, and to say that the Federal Minister for Justice is, in fact, primarily responsible for matters relating to the matter of readmission law. However, as you know – and this is also done in the response to the question – a working group has been set up in the Ministry of Justice, with the cooperation of all the bodies concerned, which is expected to present a draft law by the end of April 1997. This working group also includes the Federal Ministry of Finance and the Federal Ministry of Economic Affairs, the interest representatives and prominent legal experts. But I do not want to hide because I believe that some key points are very important. I could imagine, among other things, that a mandatory offer should be made to all minority shareholders and that the offer price for minority shareholders in certain relation to the acquisition price and the average exchange price for the last year or the last half year. I also believe that rules of conduct and fairness are necessary for purchasers and target companies, and, last but not least, that the establishment of an arbitration body that decides in case of doubt may be very essential. In the creation of such a right of acquisition, the Ministry of Finance, but also I as the Federal Minister, have great interest. This interest exists, in particular, because it fits in with the mix of measures to strengthen the Austrian capital market, which you have also mentioned. However, I want to understand that such measures need to be carefully prepared and discussed in detail. The sale of the CA shares of the federal government should not be an inconsistency and direct cause for an unbalanced legal solution not agreed with the European Union. – Thank you very much for your attention.

From my point of view, the most important thing about the written answer to this urgent question – and at the same time the subject of our massive criticism – is the definition made in question 12: that the risk of poverty risk in Austria has fallen in recent years. It's not rising, it's falling down, it's in the answer to the question! Mr State Secretary! Even if you have to stand for the Chancellor of the Federal Republic of Germany here, perhaps for a German chancellor who is not responsible for this question – I do not know – I would like to say that, after the debate in Parliament in mid-December and after the poverty conference that took place in January, and after a very broad public debate on poverty today, in February 1997, I do not think it is possible to say that the risk of poverty has fallen in recent years. You can't say that at the best of wills. Ladies and gentlemen! The risk of poverty has increased. What we have to talk about is at most the extent to which it has increased, how many groups of people actually embrace it, and how we want to define poverty and poverty threat. In the event that, after all the examples that have occurred in recent weeks and months on this issue, it should still be claimed that the risk has fallen, I would like to give you once again an example of two cases in which it can be demonstrated that this risk has not fallen but has increased. The case of one concerns a worker in Tyrol who, after a serious traffic accident, was unemployed for a long time and entered an employment project at the end of 1995, a socio-economic project that lasted one year and with which he should be mobilized for employment. – By the way, the – clamp on – successful – clamp – has succeeded: He has a spot in view, but he doesn't have it yet. He's got them in view for the middle of this year. The one-year employment project has expired, it is again unemployed – up to the date that it has not yet arrived and will be able to take up work – and is seeking unemployment support. And now the provisions laid down in the savings package make it clear that the basis for calculating unemployment benefit is the previous year or the previous year, if the application is made within the first half of the year. [[Abg. Dr. Feurstein: That can also be an advantage!]] Mr Feurstein! Do you know what that means in the specific case? – This worker no longer receives unemployment benefit in the amount in which he has received it before – this is no closer to the point, but I assume that it was between 7 000 and 12 000 S, as it corresponds to the average – but he now receives an unemployment benefit of 3 600 S, because the unemployment benefit is now calculated due to the unemployment benefit, i.e. his income in 1995 and is 60 percent of it. Ladies and gentlemen! Mr State Secretary, on behalf of the Federal Chancellor! Under these circumstances, do you want to claim that the risk has fallen?- Oh, my God! In fact, it has risen! I am going to present the case two to you because you are likely to argue that the worker has the opportunity to apply for social assistance. The case two concerns a woman in Vienna with an emergency aid cover of 12 000 p. This one she had. That was the maximum relation, and I say this in particular to the address of the liberals, because they sometimes give the impression that one could live in Austria in unemployment as in the Schlaraffenland. This woman moved 12 000 S, has a child to care for, and is 40 percent disabled. This means that the extent of their disability will certainly affect their chances of ever finding employment again. In so far as we can agree, I assume. Because of the savings package, their emergency aid has fallen from approximately 12 000 S to 7 800 S. This woman wrote us – I suppose, also to the other parties – a letter in which she asks: What do you want me to do? What do you want me to survive with? I can't pay my rent with these 7 800 S, provide a child, and at the same time look for work. That's why she's doing it. We have written her back quite helplessly: In the context of social assistance, try at least with the rental aid of the municipality of Vienna. The woman said – I telephoned with her – that, of course, she had already applied for the rental aid. It had previously received 1,200 S and, due to the reduction of emergency aid by 4000 S, received more rent interest aid on the basis of a new application by 100 S. This is a benefit from social assistance. In other words, social assistance, which is intended to offset the risk that social insurance and unemployment insurance can no longer carry, does not cover this risk. It's poverty, and it's poverty pure! The woman told me on the phone: I've been in debt for two months. I don't know how long I can stay in the apartment. I have to buy it. I can't take care of thinking about what's going on with my apartment now. – The consequence was that the municipality of Vienna also stopped the rental aid which this woman was trying to use to improve income. A clear case! But in the consequence, ladies and gentlemen, poverty. That's what we're on the real problem. By your provisions in this law, in this savings package, you have made a significant contribution in recent years to increasing the risk of poverty in Austria! If, therefore, you say in questions 20 to 23 that the balanced budget consolidation has covered all groups and evenly considered them, then you, ladies and gentlemen, miss out on the fact that the groups which were already at risk of poverty were affected or threatened by the "balanced" reduction in incomes to an unprecedented extent. That means, right now. Their argument of equal danger to all fully recognizes that from the outset there was an uneven distribution and that those who were previously poor have become even poorer by their actions. I would like to acknowledge that, in his statement before this plenary session, the Federal Chancellor, for the first time – which was also the subject of our poverty debate – has taken the word "poverty" into his mouth. It is indeed a tragedy that we are ready to see that for decades the poverty hidden in this country has not been addressed. I recognize that this has been said, but, ladies and gentlemen of the Federal Government and the Government parties, you must also take the consequences of taking the necessary measures. Recognising poverty as a problem is part of the problem, the other part of the problem, ladies and gentlemen of the government parties, with your policy of recent years that you have to answer and which you need to change in the interests of combating poverty.

First of all, we must bear in mind that we are de facto living in a very wealthy country, in which poverty has nevertheless become a realistic threat or reality for some groups of the population. In particular, I count child-rich households, single women and households where the main source of income has been lost by unemployment. But poverty is a very complex phenomenon and affects not only the mentioned but all areas of life. The fight against poverty therefore requires not only social and employment policies, but also measures in education, family, housing and fiscal policies. In concrete terms, the fight against poverty must continue to take place in an active employment policy. The central task is therefore to increase the opportunities for the labour market as a whole and those of disadvantaged groups in particular. [[Abg. Oillinger: But you don't notice that!]] That is the primary objective of this federal government. This was also recorded in the Government Declaration by German Chancellor Klima and in the comprehensive catalogue of measures presented at the Hohe Haus on the occasion of the special meeting a week ago. Therefore, I will only repeat shortly: We must start with a European employment policy, give further intensity to the expansion of infrastructure, reduce bureaucracy, force up-to-date training, strengthen exports and promote the establishment of new jobs, particularly in the services sector, as well as the creation of new areas of employment. – These are the priorities of this government, which have already been mentioned on several occasions and which I want to focus only on supporting the fight against poverty, since the approach is probably correct. There is nothing to be seen in this area, but I would like to point out that, for example, the compensation allowance for pension insurance in 1984 to 1996 has been increased seven times disproportionately, thus making a significant contribution to the provision of living in old age. While age poverty has been identified as a key problem in many studies over the past decades, recent studies show that this phenomenon has been successfully combated in Austria. In this context, attention should also be paid in particular to the introduction of care provision in Austria in 1993. I would like to say the following: A high level of employment is the best guarantee for further development of the social security system and for ensuring the financing of the social state. The fight against poverty can not only be the task of the Federal Government, but must also be promoted in the countries and communities. However, it is also a social policy task to which not only public institutions can be used. In this area, it is also important to involve the numerous private organisations that provide assistance in the daily handling of this problem, and it is up to the public to continue to support these organisations. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] High house! The phenomenon of poverty in our country has changed considerably in recent years. In addition, it is important to mobilise all the forces of society to take up this social struggle and, in particular, to reach out to those who have no means of helping themselves. In this sense, the support measures of this Federal Government will be shown.

[[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] This is reflected, among other things, by a lower interest rate differential to Germany. In the ten-year range, as you know, the interest spread to Germany, which was still at 45 to 50 basis points in the autumn of 1995, has completely disappeared – and this at a tendingly declining rate of interest. The Austrian foreign exchange reserves also show a markedly rising trend. They reached a total of 204 billion in the last week's ID of the Austrian National Bank, while at the end of 1995 only 179 billion were available. This is a clear proof of the trust of international capital markets in Austrian budget policy and in Austrian banking. This is also evident in the development of stock prices. We only reached the six-year high at the ATX yesterday, and this, although there is no longer any anonymity in the case of new commitments to shares. Nevertheless, this was the highest course. I therefore believe that there is no need to change banking secrecy at the moment. On the issue of savings book anonymity that you have attracted: This too cannot be a reason for changing banking secrecy. Austria will remain in its position that the anonymous savings books are not suitable for money laundering, and will also provide the Commission with a very good legal basis. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Dr. Graf: As before!]] Of course, I cannot say with certainty what the European Court of Justice is going to do if the Commission will turn to it. But there is no need, for example, to change banking secrecy with caution. The Austrian banking secret is to be described as a very good thing, as it is secured by Austrian law. The standardised exceptions to banking secrecy are necessary for the purposes of civil and criminal justice, but also for the prevention of tax evasion. It is also particularly important to point out that an intensification of banking secrecy in Austria in the international environment could lead to a sharper look. [[Abg. May. Stadler: Why? Reasons!]] In the international comparison, the Austrian banking secret is quite possible. Let me compare it with that of Switzerland to give a statement here. While banking secrecy in Austria has a legal basis, which can only be changed with constitutional quos, the Swiss is only partly based on law, partly only on jurisdiction, which can of course also change. The criminal security of banking secrecy in Austria and Switzerland is equivalent. The difference is simply that in Austria it is an authorisation offence, in Switzerland it is an official offence. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: And in the turn of evidence!]] In breaking through banking secrecy, it is clear that in money laundering cases – and this is a very important issue – banks in Switzerland are entitled to display, while the Austrian banks are obliged to do so, and this is a very important point in the current discussion. On the other hand, Austrian banking secrecy is better protected in the civil process. Very similar, if you really compare this serious, it is in Austria and Switzerland when handling in tax matters. The Swiss banking secret is not broken through in the event of simple tax infringements, but probably in the case of tax fraud, the Austrian banking secret only in the event of deliberate financial offences, and I believe it is quite right to break the banking secret in such cases. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: Courts should decide, not authorities!]] High House, I'll gather: At this point, we do not need to intensify banking secrecy from our point of view, and we are not taking any steps in this direction at present. Thank you.

We, the Republic of Austria, believe that this famous article 3 of the Money Laundry Directive for Austria is therefore not to be applied because we achieve this objective of combating money laundering by other means. Article 189 of the EU Treaty only says that the objective of preventing money laundering is binding, but the means used are free for individual national states. Firstly, according to the Austrian banks' declaration of due diligence, each deposit of 200 000 S or more must prove the identity, and secondly, the §§ 39 to 41 Banking Act ensures that, if there is the slightest suspicion of money laundering, the switching officer has not only determined the identity, but must be stopped immediately to understand the EDOK, the intrusion group against organized crime. These are two sufficient elements to ensure that, with all confidence in our savings culture, we nevertheless take up very energetically the fight against money laundering in Austria. Of course we confess to that. I say one thing very clearly: We will challenge this dispute before the European Court of Justice. We live in a rule of law, and the European Union is also a legal community. This is quite legitimate, otherwise we do not need an ECJ if there are different legal systems. I'll tell you one thing: I am pleased that we will win this dispute. But I also say: If we lose him, the world won't collapse. It's no reason for hysteria and for illness, ladies and gentlemen. If we really lose the process and if our legal status should not be maintained, we will intensify banking secrecy. But we should not run uncertainty and signal weakness by speeding up banking secrecy. This is the wrong policy.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e12452	Mr President, I should like to thank the President-in-Office of the Council. Mr State Secretary! High house! In the assessment of the Austrian Federal budget for 1995, whose estimated end could not be met, several factors which were not calculable in the past must be included. Because, as every federal budget, of course, has an impact on the economy, so the economic situation also has a strong impact on the budget. Furthermore, the 1995 budget assessment should take into account the increased structural adjustment requirements resulting from EU accession in 1994, the start of fiscal consolidation and the impact of the 1994 tax reform, which has a damper impact on the tax rate. It is also important not to ignore the bad international economic situation of this period. All these circumstances had a decisive impact on the 1995 budget, making it all the more difficult to achieve the indicators for a macroeconomic balance contained in § 2 of the Federal Budget Act. Although the real increase in the buttoinland product was in line with the international economy, GDP fell by 1.2% compared with 1994. In particular in the construction industry, demand and production dropped. This is certainly also due to the weather situation in 1995. Further negative effects on economic growth were the falling foreign demand in travel and the long-term low-cost flight fee travel. There has also been a significant increase in demand in the export markets which are important for Austria. Nevertheless, export activity increased by 8.1 percent in 1995, but was also relativized by the dynamic development of imports of services and direct imports. This development, together with the declines in net income from tourism, is the main cause of the negative current account, which reached an increase of 26.7 billion schilling compared with 1994. The situation in the labour market also began to deteriorate in 1995. The unemployment rate thus increased by 0.1 percent. However, this trend, which continued in 1996, seems to have finally slowed down, as was shown by the falling unemployment rate at the beginning of 1997. Another important economic indicator is the inflation rate, which rose by 2.2 percent in 1995 less than in the previous year, so that, together with the wage negotiations this year, Austrians nevertheless managed to achieve a better real income increase than in previous years. Once again, in this situation, the important stability policy function of the social partners has been demonstrated, which has strengthened Austria as an economic location with stable conditions. Finally, I would like to comment on the budget surplus of 15.6 billion schilling. First: Unfortunately, because of the economic situation I explained earlier, the budget estimate of 102 billion schilling could not be met. Less revenue of 4 billion schilling and the excess of the preliminated expenditure of around 12 billion schilling led to this difference. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! This federal accounts report shows that the government has put in place the right measures in this economically difficult period, such as consolidation measures. In addition, this federal accounts report also shows that the 1995 budget was the basis for maintaining the economic location of Austria and that the social democratic budget policy guarantees the continued existence of Austria as a financially stable country. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e12534	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! Mr Böhacker has addressed to me a question relating to the insolvency fund, which is what the further development in connection with the credit debts of this fund is about. – Deputies! To this end, the following should be said: We have repeatedly presented the debt of the insolvency fund in the federal accounts for a number of years, and we have also shown the development that results within this fund in connection with the delivery of these loans. The last figures of the year 1995 available to us can be found in the Federal Accounts. At the end of 1995, these loans amounted to 6 billion schillings. However, we do not have any more recent figures – I have collected the latest information. The figures for 1996 will not be available until April 1997, so that I can't tell you more up-to-date figures at the moment. I can assure you, however, that the Court of Auditors will, of course, obtain this material and, in the next federal accounts, will, of course, be presented again, because it has done its job to review the objectives of the Federal Government, namely to reduce the debt of the Fund by the end of 1998, and to present it in the respective federal accounts. Their second question, Mr deputies, was how the Court of Auditors held in connection with its request that those difficulties arising from the entry into force of § 65a of the Federal Budget Act should be solved. Deputies! I would like to say the following: With the newly introduced § 65a of the Federal Budget Act, the possibility was created to carry out credit operations, i.e. financial debt acquisitions, already in the current financial year, which can only be calculated in advance in the next financial year. On the other hand, the resulting debts, namely the obligations to repay, are in accordance with § 52 para. 2 of the Federal Budget Act already in the current year as financial debt to bring forward-looking accounts. As a result of this newly introduced provision, technical difficulties arise. For if the debts under § 65a of the Federal Budget Act are to be attributed only to the next financial year, the proceeds from these financial debts must be proved as pro-forma debt by means of the passive accounting delimitation. In accordance with § 52 para. 5 of the Federal Budget Act, however, the financial liabilities arising from these proceeds are recorded as forward-looking debt in the current financial year, in order not to have to double-prove these debts, these amounts are to be corrected again by the active deduction. This is therefore a rather complicated process, which we believe should be solved in a simpler way, by amending § 52 para. 5 of the Federal Budget Act takes place in such a way that the receipts of debt, i.e. the proceeds, and the proof of debt, are already presented in the current financial year in which the proceeds flow in cash. In our view, this problem would be most easy to solve in this way. We know, however, that the Ministry of Finance has not yet given its assent. We have, however, discussed the problem in a very detailed way in this Federal Accounting Board – I have just described it very briefly now – and have thought that we should approach the legislator with this proposal. We have therefore done this, and I have done so even more closely. Several Members, including you, Mr Van der Bellen, have discussed the problem of the introduction of reserves. Deputies! I should like to say the following: We have repeatedly identified the problems arising from the introduction of reserves in the federal accounts in recent years, because, in our view, there is a difference between purely budgetary considerations and economic considerations. For it is not quite easy now to want to explain to someone that a rescheduling can be calculated as an issue, but a withdrawal of reserves as a revenue. However, I would like to make it clear to you that, just as we did this this time in the federal accounts, we did not have the intention of concealing or bagatelizing something, but we have only presented the budgetary account as it appears at the outset of the reserves and have also made a proposal in the context, but elsewhere in the federal accounts, to the effect that we could perhaps make a contribution to dealing with this problem or to providing more transparency. The Court of Auditors' proposal in this context is that the turnover of the compensatory reserve should be included in the compensatory budget, where we think it would be more appropriate. That would solve this problem. We have not yet received any approval from the Ministry of Finance. However, we have allowed ourselves to retain this proposal in the federal accounts. Deputies! In addition, they have suggested to the Court of Auditors that it should make more analytical comments either in the introduction or in the summary. We are quite open to such a suggestion, and we are of course aware that, in connection with the objective set by the Federal Government, there is now also a special reason for bringing this problem closer to the honourable Member, but also to the public. However, I would like to point out that we adopted the problem of how the Maastricht criteria were in place in 1995. You could have given the impression that we had not said anything about this because of your speech, Mr Deputies. In two parts of the federal accounts, our remarks are that the re-indebtedness at the end of 1995 amounted to 6.2 per cent and the total debt ratio was 69.5 per cent, so that the Maastricht criteria were clearly missed in 1995. [[President Dr Neisser shall take over the chairmanship.]] In addition, under Chapter 7 of the Federal Accounts, we have also referred to the fact that we have very much to distinguish between the fulfilment of the Maastricht criteria on the one hand and the consolidation of the budget on the other. For if one might think that these are two completely identical problems, there is no complete parity with regard to the calculation of the Maastricht criteria mentioned by you, as far as the financial debt under the Federal Budget Act is concerned. That is why we believe - and have felt like bringing it closer to the legislator - that, regardless of the fulfilment of the Maastricht criteria, we should also look at how the budget consolidation is going to take place. You have also referred to the fact that, in your contribution to the discussion, there are certain formal scopes which do not exist under federal budgetary law in the context of the fulfilment of the Maastricht criteria. We have also referred to this problem or this disparity in the federal accounts, and we therefore believe that we have fulfilled the obligation of the Court of Auditors to do so. However, we would like to welcome your suggestion, Mr Member, to continue to deal with this problem in the next federal accounts and to bring further details. However, I should also like to point out that the Court of Auditors, as regards the fulfilment of the Maastricht criteria, has already given its opinion to the National Council in its report form, most recently in detail in the 1994 Report of Action on the Administrative Year, which was presented to the National Council at the end of 1995 and which refers to the differences in the calculation of the Maastricht criteria, on the one hand, compared to the normal financial debt report or the normal financial debt ratio, on the other hand, as defined by the Federal Budget Act. In this activity report, we have also thought of the role of the countries and communities which have to fulfil their contribution in connection with the Maastricht criteria – as we know, according to the current determinations of 0.3 percent – and we will continue to observe very closely during this year, as will not only the Federal Government, but also the countries and the communities of these commitments. Deputies! I hope to agree with you in this respect that much has been done by the Court of Auditors in this regard. But I can come back to your suggestion: We will be very happy to receive them and report to the National Council even more about them in the next federal accounts.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e15588	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! The Federal Act on the acquisition of part-time use rights in non-movable things, in short referred to as part-time use law, has the main purpose of providing better information to the acquirer and, above all, better protection.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e15610	I would ask for a little more attention or at least a little more rest for the honourable Members. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e15625	Part-time rights can be acquired, for example, from apartments, resorts or also from hotel rooms. Part-time use of holiday facilities is better known as time-sharing. They have become increasingly popular in recent years. There are several forms. As an example, I would like to mention exchange pools. Exchange pools allow a person with part - time Austrian ownership, for example, to exchange with an Australian part - time owner. This opens up opportunities that are of course gladly used and that kind of holiday making still beautify and increase the attractiveness. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Dr. Ofner.]] Especially in the construction and tourism sector, where economic crime also occurs, there are not often dubious figures and even companies when it comes to making the quick money for something new, so to speak, and thus also to taking advantage of non-thinkings. Another problem is that even behind the operators, non-transparent organizations do not rarely hide. Furthermore, operators often make high debts, resulting in insolvency, and the acquirer comes to his money. It is also complained that accounts are often made on the basis of very favourable offers, but that the buyer is not sufficiently clear that the running operating costs can be very high. Sometimes these are in no relation to the value of the services offered. So you can say that the time-sharing has come up with a little talk. It is therefore very important and necessary for this area to be regulated now, for the benefit of the acquirers in order to introduce a rule on abuse and crime in the area of part-time use of holiday objects. [[Congratulation with the SPÖ]] The European Parliament has already recognised these consumer protection deficiencies in the late 1980s and has called on the Commission to adopt a directive. This Directive on the protection of acquirers with regard to certain aspects of contracts on the acquisition of part-time rights, particularly real estate, was also presented by the Commission after years and was adopted by the European Parliament and the European Council in October 1994. The purpose of this Directive, which has now been valid for the EEA as a whole since 1995, is to provide a minimum basis for common rules in this area which ensure the proper functioning of the internal market and also the protection of acquisitions. The main contents of this Directive and, of course, of this law are, in addition to definitions and the definition of the scope, the rules set out in 14 sections – for example, on pre-contractual information obligations, on the form, content and language of the use contract. The right of withdrawal is also very important. The Directive explicitly leaves Member States open to adopt or maintain rules that are even more advantageous in the view of the protection of acquisitions. The 30-month deadline for the implementation of this Directive shall end for the Member States on 29 March. ==References== High house! Ladies and gentlemen! As long as your consent is granted, we can comply with this deadline. I assume that all the political groups in the committee have already agreed to this law. Unfortunately, we were unable to participate in the discussions on this directive at the time, since Austria was not yet a member of the European Union when it was adopted. The draft law now before us is, of course, characterised by the existing EU directive, but some rules leave the directive completely to Member States, such as the form of the declaration of withdrawal, the detailed provisions on the resolution of the credit agreement or the sanctioning of infringements of the standards of the Directive. Some of the regulatory content of the Directive had to be brought into line with the Austrian legal system, harmonised with existing provisions and, of course, coordinated in terms of time. This applies particularly to parts of the Consumer Protection Act. It was discussed, for example, whether the provisions of the Directive should not be incorporated into the Consumer Protection Act. However, it has been abandoned because it is a very special area, which is regulated here, and the Consumer Protection Act would otherwise be overlaid. This is a complex matter, which is regulated by more than twelve paragraphs. § 10 of this Act is not specified by the Directive, but also provides – optionally however – basic safeguards – important and useful whenever problems arise, for example when a company becomes insolvent or becomes insolvent. The use of such security, such as the trustee mortgage, is not least due to the different time-sharing systems in the full autonomy of the Contracting Parties. ParlaMint-AT_1997-02-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00064_d7e15762	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The present draft law regulates the time-sharing for the first time in Austria, although the term does not appear at all in the draft law. Nevertheless, behind this term there are a number of manifestations, but ultimately it is about the exploitation and marketing of apartments. According to the principle, the customer acquires the right to use a holiday home or a part of an apartment exclusively for a period of a year, but the legal constructions can be varied. The range ranges from fruit enjoyment, co-ownership, issue of shares, club systems and trust models to rental and accommodation contracts. The economic importance of these part-time contracts is quite different internationally. In Austria, which I take from a daily newspaper, 3 to 5 percent of all beds are currently offered as part of the time-sharing, which is 30,000 beds. However, there is an increase in urban areas, for example in Vienna, Bregenz and Klagenfurt, while the number of time-sharing systems stagnates in other areas of Austria. Germany has seen an increase of 5 percent over the last three years. In other holiday areas, in overseas, for example in the Caribbean, this distribution was increased by around 270 percent. So we see that there is a real need for action. This need for action has increased in the light of the fact that over recent years aggressive sales practices have given rise to undue and overly fast contracts, resulting in damage to the acquirers. My predecessor pointed out: The EU has responded with a directive and requires its Member States to transpose this EU Directive into national law. The main content of this EU Directive includes the information requirement of the provider, the right of withdrawal, especially of the acquirer, and, ultimately, the rules on the form and content of the use contract. Today's submission of the Austrian Part-Time Use Act follows in much of the EU Directive, but goes far beyond that. From this point of view, I have repeatedly pointed out in other contexts that, in Austria, we must take care not to make Austrian providers, Austrian standard users worse than that in the EU area. That's why the spagate was to be created. In the specific case, this means that it was necessary to prevent excessive arrangements in order not to reach the final conclusion for the sale of part-time contracts in Austria. On the other hand, our tourism industry needs this new form of financing to improve its difficult situation. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Against this background, the draft presented seems to me to be balanced. On the one hand, it significantly improves consumer protection and, on the other, contributes significantly to legal certainty, but also to the competitiveness of Austrian suppliers. I do not want to repeat the content of the Part-Time Use Act. It ranges from precise provisions in the information document to the form and content of the contract. The language of the contract is of particular importance because the acquirer should not have a disadvantage due to lack of language skills. However, the central part of this draft is the right of withdrawal, whereby the acquirer is granted a right of withdrawal within a certain period of time without grounds of withdrawal. I have already mentioned that the Austrian Part-Time Use Act goes beyond the EU Directive. One of these areas is the optional guarantee. The basic right is granted to introduce a real burden on the operation of the part-time use facility, and the user should also have the right to provide the entrepreneur with active action. These are the following: If the user has acquired an investment by purchase contract in which golf, tennis, pool and so on is offered to him, he should be able to force the entrepreneur to offer these services in the future. In addition, it is possible to file a trustee mortgage so that the acquirer can also safely recover the payments made. These additional safeguards contribute, on the one hand, to the standard of protection for the To further improve acquisitions, but they are also suitable to raise the reputation of the time-sharing system as a whole. This possibility can enable the Austrian provider to be more competitive with foreign providers because it has an additional advertising argument in hand. My predecessor has already pointed out that some clarifications and improvements have been made in the Committee. I should like to make a brief amendment, which makes two minor clarifications: [[Quoted print version removed]] I conclude by quoting the draft Part-Time Use Act from the opinion of UNI Graz as a Styrian Member: All in all, this draft is very carefully prepared. These are independent Austrian solutions, to which hopefully the well-deserved pan-European model effect will be attributed. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! We can fully endorse this judgment by the Austrian People's Party and will therefore be very pleased to give our consent to this draft.

In many cases, aggressive distribution methods or misleading advertising material were used to avoid important information, such as the legal nature of the rights acquired or the actual cost burden. Moreover, most cross-border contracts have raised difficult conflict-law issues, and customers have often been confronted with the applicability of an entirely unknown right. The EU has adopted this problem in the conviction that a high national consumer protection standard must also remain ineffective if a applicable other law does not provide such a level of protection. In my view, this EU directive is a particularly good example of the progress made by the EU in consumer protection in contrast to occasional unknowings. It is not only that today's substantial impetus to the development of consumer protection is based on the EU itself that the harmonisation of a high consumer protection standard in the EU is a guarantee of protection, even in relation to national borders. This draft law, which has already been pointed out, is not merely a schematic implementation of the directive on timely implementation. Some of the provisions of the Directive have been modified and extended in line with the system in order to ensure that consumer protection is as close as possible. Overall, this law establishes a rounded, balanced consumer protection system for the special field of time-sharing. The most important innovation in practice is the approximation of the basic book survey to the company book survey that has been proven for years now in § 6 of the Basic Book Transfer Act. In future, therefore, the basic book survey should be possible for everyone without needing a modest authorisation. Also through the revision of § 469a ABGB, which simplifies the basic book treatment of the non-reservable cancellation obligation, the basic book is relieved of unnecessary registrations, thus making it clearer and more user-friendly.
